personal / …) resolve automatically\nper repository.\n\n| Recipe | Represents | Provenance |\n|--------|------------|------------|\n| [`ssh-config.recipe`](./ssh-config.recipe) | `~/.ssh/config` | gist [`2c98cff…`](https://gist.github.com/castocolina/2c98cff5920aff0f528ca847f56d7627) |\n| [`gitconfig.recipe`](./gitconfig.recipe) | `~/.gitconfig` | gist [`60f2f1d…`](https://gist.github.com/castocolina/60f2f1d08c38eb9bd59e61e7e3ee0f5e) |\n\nLegacy starting point: `~/git/personal/scripts/common/ssh-keygen.sh`.\n\n## What the recipes establish (the wiring gitid must reproduce)\n\n1. **SSH alias per identity** — `Host <identity>.<provider>` (e.g. `personal.github.com`)\n   with `Hostname ssh.github.com`, `Port 443` (alt-SSH to bypass firewalls),\n   `User git`, an explicit `IdentityFile`, and `IdentitiesOnly yes`.\n2. **Git identity selection by remote URL** — the *primary* match is\n   `[includeIf \"hasconfig:remote.*.url:git@<alias>:*/**\"] → path = ~/.gitconfig_<identity>`,\n   so cloning `git@personal.github.com:you/repo` loads the right identity. A\n   `gitdir:~/…/` match is shown only as an *alternative*.\n3. **URL rewriting** — `[url \"git@<provider>:\"] insteadOf = https://<provider>/`\n   forces SSH over HTTPS.\n4. **Per-identity fragment** — name, email, signing key, `commit.gpgsign`.\n\n## Caveat: structure, not key type\n\nThe gists predate the project and use RSA (`id_rsa_*`). `gitid` supersedes this\nwith **one ed25519 key per identity** (authentication + commit signing via\n`gpg.format=ssh` + `~/.ssh/allowed_signers`). Take the *structure* from these\nrecipes — aliases, port 443, `IdentitiesOnly yes`, `includeIf gitdir:`/`hasconfig:`,\n`insteadOf` — never the key algorithm.","lineStart":1,"lineEnd":36,"totalLines":36,"truncated":false}},"title":"recipes/README.md","time":{"start":1787497573716,"end":1787497581788}},"id":"prt_02f286d4f001wkiwWrXKmUvPui","sessionID":"ses_fd0d7e845ffePr6WJm6HasDnQo","messageID":"msg_02f282f62001f40XATF6rzcxCR"}}

Phase 3 introduces no new role.\n83: \n84: | Role | TUI treatment | Web mirror (reference only) | Usage |\n85: |------|----------------|------------------------------|-------|\n86: | Label | `Bold(true)` | `roles.label`, `fontWeight: 700` | field labels, always bold in every state |\n87: | Field (value) | plain, no styling | `roles.field`, visible border | the value itself |\n88: | Field — focused | `Foreground(ANSI 4) + Bold`, NO border (D1) | `roles.focusedField` — accent tint, no layout change | value + `▸` marker + bracket delimiters |\n89: | Field — blurred | `Faint(true)` | `roles.blurredField`, opacity 0.85 | dim brackets/value, unchanged single row |\n90: | Hint | `Faint(true)` | `roles.hint` | helper/explanation copy, never collapses |\n91: | Warning | `Foreground(ANSI 3)` — yellow | `roles.warning` | advisory / needs-action states, incl. no user input\n\tenv := append(os.Environ(), \"HOME=\"+home, \"TERM=xterm-256color\")\n\tif fakeSSHDir != \"\" {\n\t\tenv = append(env, \"PATH=\"+fakeSSHDir+\":\"+os.Getenv(\"PATH\"))\n\t}\n\tcmd.Env = env\n\treturn cmd\n}\n\n// openCreateWizard boots the real binary, waits for the shell to render, and\n// presses 'n' to open the create wizard from the identities pane (works\n// whether or not any identity already exists — 'n' is unconditional).\nfunc openCreateWizard(t *testing.T, s *ptySession) {\n\tt.Helper()\n\tuiReady(t, s)\n\ts.sendKey([]byte(\"n\"), keystrokeDelay)\n\tmustSee(t, s, \"Step 1/4\", \"wizard: step 0 (SSH details) opens\")\n}\n\n// quitCleanly sends q then Enter (the real top-level quit confirmation) and\n// waits for the process to exit, matching the graceful-quit pattern already\n// proven in dummy_demo_e2e_test.go — avoids relying on close()'s best-effort\n// ctrl+c for tests that want to assert a clean (0) exit.\nfunc quitCleanly(t *testing.T, s *ptySession) {\n\tt.Helper()\n\twaitCh := make(chan error, 1)\n\tgo func() { waitCh <- s.cmd.Wait() }()\n\ts.sendKey([]byte(\"q\"), keystrokeDelay)\n\ts.sendKey(dummyKeyEnter, keystrokeDelay)\n\tselect {\n\tcase werr := <-waitCh:\n\t\tif werr != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"quit: process exited with error: %v\", werr)\n\t\t}\n\tcase <-time.After(10 * time.Second):\n\t\tt.Fatal(\"quit: process did not exit within 10s of q + Enter\")\n\t}\n}\n\n// seedEncryptedKeyFixture writes a real ed25519 private key (optionally\n// passphrase-encrypted) plus its `.pub` sibling at keyPath and returns the\n// generated authorized-key line. Last frame:\\n%s\", label, last)\n\t}\n\ts.sendKey([]byte(fmt.Sprintf(\"\\x1b[<0;%d;%dM\", col, row)), keystrokeDelay) // SGR press\n\ts.sendKey([]byte(fmt.Sprintf(\"\\x1b[<0;%d;%dm\", col, row)), keystrokeDelay) // SGR release\n}\n\n// tabKeys sends n literal Tab bytes, each followed by keystrokeDelay — the\n// wizard's step-0/step-2 focus rings both move via Tab (identities.go\n// handleWizardKey \"tab\", \"down\" case).\nfunc tabKeys(s *ptySession, n int) {\n\tfor i := 0; i < n; i++ {\n\t\ts.sendKey([]byte(\"\\t\"), keystrokeDelay)\n\t}\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// 1. <-s.stopCh:\n\t\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif rerr != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}()\n\n\t// Goroutine B: capability-response drainer (see startPTY notes).\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tdefer close(s.drainDone)\n\t\tresp := make([]byte, 256)\n\t\tfor {\n\t\t\tn, rerr := emu.Read(resp)\n\t\t\tif n > 0 {\n\t\t\t\t_, _ = ptmx.Write(resp[:n]) //nolint:errcheck // best-effort feed-back\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif rerr != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}()\n\n\t// Goroutine C: event loop — sole owner of emu.\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tdefer close(s.done)\n\t\tfor {\n\t\t\tselect Goroutine B (response drainer): drains emu.Read() — the emulator's internal\n//     io.PipeReader — and writes the terminal-capability responses back to ptmx.\n//     Without this drainer, emu.Write() blocks when Bubble Tea issues DA/DECRQM\n//     queries whose responses must flow back to the app.\n//\n//  3. Goroutine C (event loop): receives PTY chunks and feeds them to emu.Write(),\n//     and answers snapshot requests from the test via reqCh.\n//\n// emu is owned exclusively by goroutine C. No other goroutine calls emu.Write(),\n// emu.String(), etc. This single-owner design is the only reliable way to prevent\n// the deadlock: (goroutine A holds mutex + blocks in emu.Write → io.PipeWriter,\n// goroutine B blocks in mutex.Lock called by snapshot()).\nfunc startPTY(t *testing.T, cmd *exec.Cmd) *ptySession {\n\tt.Helper()\n\temu := vt.NewEmulator(ptyTermWidth, ptyTermHeight)\n\n\tws := &pty.Winsize{Rows: ptyTermHeight, Cols: ptyTermWidth}\n\tptmx, err := pty.StartWithSize(cmd, ws) //nolint:gosec // arg-slice; binary path from BuildBinary\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"startPTY: pty.StartWithSize: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// emu.InputPipe() returns the emulator's internal *io.PipeWriter. Closing it\n\t// (CloseWithError) is the io.Pipe-safe way to unblock the drainer's blocking\n\t// emu.Read() at shutdown WITHOUT calling emu.Close() — the latter writes the\n\t// unsynchronised emu.closed bool that races with the drainer's Read (the pipe\n\t// itself is concurrency-safe; the bool is not).\n\temuPipe, ok := emu.InputPipe().(*io.PipeWriter)\n\tif !ok {\n\t\tt.Fatalf(\"startPTY: emu.InputPipe() is %T, want *io.PipeWriter\", emu.InputPipe())\n\t}\n\n\ts := &ptySession{\n\t\tptmx:      ptmx,\n\t\tcmd:       cmd,\n\t\treqCh:     make(chan snapshotReq, 4),\n\t\tstopCh:    make(chan struct{}),\n\t\tdone:      make(chan struct{}),\n\t\tdrainDone: make(chan struct{}),\n\t\temuPipe:   emuPipe,\n\t}\n\n\tptyCh := make(chan []byte, 128) // buffered to decouple blocking Read from event loop\n\n\t// Goroutine A: PTY reader — reads raw PTY output and forwards to event loop.\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tbuf := make([]byte, 4096)\n\t\tfor {\n\t\t\tn, rerr := ptmx.Read(buf)\n\t\t\tif n > 0 {\n\t\t\t\tcp := make([]byte, n)\n\t\t\t\tcopy(cp, buf[:n])\n\t\t\t\tselect {\n\t\t\t\tcase ptyCh <- cp:\n\t\t\t\tcase <-s.stopCh:\n\t\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif rerr != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}()\n\n\t// Goroutine B: response drainer — drains emu.Read() (the emulator's internal\n\t// PipeReader) so emu.Write() never blocks on a full pipe. Responses are fed\n\t// back to the PTY master so the Bubble Tea app receives its terminal-capability\n\t// replies (DA, DECRQM, etc.).\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tdefer close(s.drainDone)\n\t\tresp := make([]byte, 256)\n\t\tfor {\n\t\t\tn, rerr := emu.Read(resp)\n\t\t\tif n > 0 {\n\t\t\t\t_, _ = ptmx.Write(resp[:n]) //nolint:errcheck // best-effort feed-back\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif rerr != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn // io.EOF once emuPipe is CloseWithError'd in close()\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}()\n\n\t// Goroutine C: event loop — sole owner of emu.\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tdefer close(s.done)\n\t\tfor {\n\t\t\tselect {\n\t\t\tcase chunk := <-ptyCh:\n\t\t\t\t_, _ = emu.Write(chunk) //nolint:errcheck // vt.Emulator.Write always returns nil err\n\t\t\tcase req := <-s.reqCh:\n\t\t\t\treq.resp <- emu.String()\n\t\t\tcase <-s.stopCh:\n\t\t\t\t// Do NOT call emu.Close() here: it writes the unsynchronised\n\t\t\t\t// emu.closed bool concurrently with the drainer's emu.Read().\n\t\t\t\t// close() unblocks the drainer via emuPipe.CloseWithError instead,\n\t\t\t\t// after this event loop (the sole emu.Write caller) has exited.\n\t\t\t\treturn\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}()\n\n\treturn s\n}\n\n// close terminates the session: sends ctrl+c to the process, stops the event\n// loop, closes the PTY master, and waits for all goroutines to drain.\nfunc (s *ptySession) close(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Helper()\n\t// Send ctrl+c to terminate the TUI gracefully (best-effort).\n\t_, _ = s.ptmx.Write([]byte{0x03}) //nolint:errcheck\n\t// Give the process a moment to handle the signal — bounded, so a\n\t// process that (for whatever reason: a sandboxed PTY not delivering\n\t// the interrupt byte's signal semantics, a hung render loop, …) never\n\t// reacts to ctrl+c cannot hang test cleanup forever. Escalates to a\n\t// hard kill only after the grace period; a healthy process that\n\t// handles ctrl+c exits well within it and this never fires.\n\twaitDone := make(chan struct{})\n\tgo func() {\n\t\t_ = s.cmd.Wait() //nolint:errcheck // best-effort cleanup; the test's own assertions already ran\n\t\tclose(waitDone)\n\t}()\n\tselect {\n\tcase <-waitDone:\n\tcase <-time.After(5 * time.Second):\n\t\tif s.cmd.Process != nil {\n\t\t\t_ = s.cmd.Process.Kill() //nolint:errcheck // best-effort — Wait() below still reaps it\n\t\t}\n\t\t<-waitDone\n\t}\n\t// Stop the event loop (sole emu.Write caller) first.\n\tclose(s.stopCh)\n\t<-s.done // wait for event loop to exit — no more emu.Write after this\n\t// Now unblock the drainer's blocking emu.Read() via the io.Pipe-safe path.\n\t// This avoids emu.Close()'s unsynchronised closed-bool write racing the Read.\n\t_ = s.emuPipe.CloseWithError(io.EOF)\n\t<-s.drainDone // wait for the drainer to exit\n\ts.ptmx.Close()\n}\n\n// sendKey writes raw bytes to the PTY master, followed by a brief sleep to let\n// the TUI process and render the input before the next keystroke.\n// raw MUST be a static literal — never derived from user input (gosec G204 clean).\nfunc (s *ptySession) sendKey(raw []byte, delay time.Duration) {\n\t_, _ = s.ptmx.Write(raw) //nolint:errcheck // best-effort\n\ttime.Sleep(delay)\n}\n\n// snapshot returns the current decoded text of the emulator (no ANSI codes).\n// It sends a request to the event loop goroutine (sole owner of emu) and waits\n// for the response. This is safe to call from the test goroutine at any time.\nfunc (s *ptySession) snapshot() string {\n\treq := snapshotReq{resp: make(chan string, 1)}\n\tselect {\n\tcase s.reqCh <- req:\n\tcase <-s.done:\n\t\treturn \"\"\n\t}\n\tselect {\n\tcase text := <-req.resp:\n\t\treturn text\n\tcase <-s.done:","lineStart":65,"lineEnd":244,"totalLines":419,"truncated":true}},"title":"e2e/ui_pty_e2e_test.go","time":{"start":1787497727650,"end":1787497730375}},"id":"prt_02f2ac69d001LI1lAdaHaFpkHn","sessionID":"ses_fd0d7e845ffePr6WJm6HasDnQo","messageID":"msg_02f2aaa210015rap3fOJwSaALT"}}
